# Claims report

Semantic hash: `0946be6d63b2a4`. Generated by Exegra from the semantic model. Do not edit; regenerate.

## How to read this report

Verified means checked by Exegra against this model revision within the stated scope and assumptions. Supported means resting on external evidence that Exegra did not derive from the model. A declared claim is not thereby true. A simulation of the model is not evidence about deployed software.

## Check and grounding

The check says how strong the evaluation behind a status is (holds-by-construction and restated-scenario cannot fail on a correct model; single-trace runs hand-written scenarios only); the grounding says whether what it reads is the platform's own entities (bound) or only its own inputs (self-contained). See docs/claims-and-evidence.md, "Check strength and grounding".

## specification-copies-fresh-and-related

Every copy of a project's specification text that Exegra declares (the session model, the command line's project, the generated documents) states a freshness bound, and its holder is related to the specification file through declared relationships; the shared copy holds no principal data.

- Kind: structural
- Method: data-model
- **Status**: Inconclusive
- Check: data-model: Decided over the records and copies of the model by the data rules.
- Grounding: bound: Reads every record and copy of the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.structural/0.1
- Scope: Checked the 32 records and 23 copies at this model revision against rules freshness-bound, holder-reaches-owner, no-principal-in-shared (items checked per rule: freshness-bound 23, holder-reaches-owner 45, no-principal-in-shared 2). Rule no-principal-in-shared read only the personal markers the model declares (a field's "personal" value or a reference to the principal): a field that holds personal data but declares neither is not seen, so the absence of a finding shows that the declared markers are absent from the copy, not that it holds no personal data.
- Explanation: No finding: Every copy states a freshness bound. A copy's holder is the owner of each record it copies, contains it or is contained in it, or a directed relationship path leads from one to the other. No shared copy holds a field that refers to the principal or is personal, or depends on authority (D-136). However, the assumption "extracted-facts-current" is recorded as refuted (the import scan found 14 file-system imports with no declared relationship: src/core/derivation-cache.js (derivation-cache) imports node:fs (filesystem); src/core/lean.js (lean-call-boundary) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/build-engine.js (rust-engine-oracle) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/check-spec.js (spec-checker) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/conformance.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/differential.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/gen-engine-inputs.js (formal-semantics) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/make-benchmark.js (benchmark-generator) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/prove-integrity.js (integrity-rules) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/prove.js (proof-export) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/record-goldens.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/rust-engine.js (rust-engine-oracle) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/spec-store.js (spec-store) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js (dev-server-plugin) imports node:fs (filesystem)), so the claim is undecided: it may still hold, but what it rests on does not.

**Holds only if**

## integrity-rules-evaluated

Integrity rules are part of the specification and are evaluated on every model: each holds with the number of instances checked, or is violated with its counterexamples (bindings, entity ids, lines); an invalid rule is a validation error, a rule over nothing is reported vacuous, and every rule of the fixture can be made to fail by a stated mutation.

## integrity-proved-in-lean

## implementation-machine-readiness-rules

A behavior declared with level "implementation" is reported with an error for each rule it breaks, and for none when it breaks none: every variable has a resolving repr; instances are {key, storedIn} with key a key field of that record; the machine says which record holds it (storage {record, key} with key a key field of the record, or instances.storedIn, and the two agree when both are present); every variable is held in a field of that record (storage.fields, default the field of its name) whose type fits its repr, every storage.fields entry names a variable and a field of the record, and no field holds two things; a stored state has a required string or enum field (storage.state, default "state") whose values, through storage.stateValues when given, distinctly cover every state; a machine with storage.creates true fills every required field of its record, while a non-creating machine is not held to this; every event field read is a declared payload field and every payload type resolves; every emit names its receiver and its event a delivery; a transaction is declared; no transition is faultOnly; every elapsed event is emitted by a timer; transitions with the same state and event are explicitly ordered (guard or otherwise on the last, otherwise only on the last); every non-terminal state has an outcome or an ignore for every event of the machine. The rules are the ready.* codes of docs/semantics.md; a machine is keyed by the whole key of its record, every key field in order, in storage.key and in instances.key (storage.key-whole, WG-4907).

- Kind: invariant
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Inconclusive
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1
- Scope: No model evaluation was performed. This rests on 12 evidence entities (ev-readiness-rules, ev-readiness-fixed, ev-readiness-model-level, ev-readiness-shipped-projects, ev-readiness-storage-alone, ev-readiness-storedin-alone, ev-readiness-stateless-exempt, ev-readiness-non-creating, ev-readiness-storage-defaults, ev-readiness-field-fit, ev-storage-key-whole, ev-storage-key-whole-list), of which 11 have a recorded execution result; the rest is author-entered.
- Explanation: Exegra holds a recorded result of executing ev-readiness-rules, ev-readiness-fixed, ev-readiness-model-level, ev-readiness-storage-alone, ev-readiness-storedin-alone, ev-readiness-stateless-exempt, ev-readiness-non-creating, ev-readiness-storage-defaults, ev-readiness-field-fit, ev-storage-key-whole and ev-storage-key-whole-list: pass. Test evidence "ev-readiness-shipped-projects" did not run (every matching test (1) skipped itself or is a todo, so nothing was executed). A claim is as strong as its weakest part, so it is not established.

## machine-realisation-and-durability

## faults-realisable-by-runtime

In a scenario that runs an implementation-level machine, a fault the generated runtime cannot produce is reported with fault.unrealisable: any event fault on an event a timer of the scenario fires (or an elapsed event); drop-event, lose-event or a delay-event by other than a sum of retry backoff waits on an at-least-once event; delay-event, retry-event or duplicate-event on an at-most-once event; lose-event or retry-event on an event that declares no delivery; enable-transition or disable-transition on a transition of an implementation-level machine. ## integrity-relations-least-model

For a program of derived relations that the Lean stratification check wf accepts and the checker solve solves, the solved table is exactly the least-model meaning Least: a tuple is in it iff it is in every interpretation that agrees with the lower strata and is closed under the clauses of its stratum.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: lean-theorem
- **Status**: Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code
- Check: lean-theorem: A theorem of the Lean core, "Exegra.Integrity.least_eq_table", checked by the Lean kernel; it covers every input.
- Grounding: bound: States a property of the Lean definitions "Exegra.Integrity.Least", "Exegra.Integrity.solve", "Exegra.Integrity.wf".
- Evaluator: lean.kernel/4.35.0
- Scope: Checked by Lean 4.35.0 at 2026-11-28T13:20:18.726Z, as recorded: the theorem's proof re-checked by the kernel and its axioms collected. It establishes the statement about the Lean definitions it names. That the code which runs is those definitions rests on the trusted base: the Lean compiler and runtime, the WebAssembly toolchain and the JS glue (the claim's assumptions).
- Explanation: "Exegra.Integrity.least_eq_table" is a theorem; the Lean kernel accepted its proof again; it depends only on propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound; its statement is about Exegra.Integrity.Least, Exegra.Integrity.solve, Exegra.Integrity.wf (statement hash 98da1a78a065).

**Holds only if**

- `trusted-lean-kernel`: The Lean 4 kernel of the toolchain formal/lean-toolchain pins (v4.35.0) accepts only well-typed proof terms.

**Counterexample**

None.

**Evidence**

None is attached.

## machine-singleton-key

An implementation-level machine that creates its records (storage.creates), has no instances.key and is run by a scenario is reported with machine.singleton-key when a key field of its record has a type with no fixed value for the one instance the runtime creates at the start: only string, uuid or an integer, or an id or scalar over one, in a field that is not optional, has one. A machine keyed by instances.key, or whose key field has such a type, is not.

- Method: external-evidence

## abstraction-structure-well-formed

Every hierarchy of Exegra's specification (logical, from exegra down to the module elements, and runtime placement) is laminar and acyclic, every module element has exactly one owner, and every declared cut is an antichain covering every element, so its abstraction map sends each element at or below it to exactly one block.

- Kind: structural
- Method: abstraction-well-formed
- **Status**: Verified within the stated model and assumptions
- Check: structural: Decided over the whole declared component graph by "abstraction-well-formed".
- Grounding: bound: Reads the declared components and relationships of the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.structural/0.1
- Scope: Checked 2 hierarchies (logical, runtime) over 446 elements and 8 cuts (l0, l1, persistence-expanded, semantic-core-expanded, module-level, runtime-hosts, runtime-platforms, runtime-placed) at this model revision, module elements derived from implementation.modules.
- Explanation: Every hierarchy is laminar and acyclic, every module element has exactly one owner, and each cut is an antichain covering every element whose abstraction map sends each element at or below it to exactly one block.

**Holds only if**

No assumptions are declared.

**Counterexample**

None.

**Evidence**

None is attached.

## cut-maps-compose

For cuts C finer than D of one hierarchy, the abstraction map of D equals the map from C to D composed with the abstraction map of C, so zooming through an intermediate level agrees with zooming directly; C refines D exactly when D opens nothing C does not, and the cut command prints a cut's blocks and element-to-block map.

## core-independent-of-ui

At cut l1 no block outside the semantic core is reached from the semantic core by a dependency relationship (depends-on, imports, invokes, composes, notifies, exercises, subscribes, reads, writes or submits-edits), directly or through any chain of them, except the JSONC parser library and the JavaScript runtime, which the core uses on purpose. The targets are everything outside the semantic core at that level, so a new top-level element is covered without being listed.

- Kind: structural
- Method: no-relationship
- **Status**: Inconclusive
- Check: structural: Decided over the whole declared component graph by "no-relationship".
- Grounding: bound: The relationships it reads are declared by authors (325 declared); nothing checked them against the code.
- Evaluator: exegra.structural/0.1
- Scope: Checked the 72 derived interactions of cut "l1" (21 blocks, hierarchy logical) of kinds depends-on, imports, invokes, composes, notifies, exercises, subscribes, reads, writes, submits-edits, from 1 source block (98 components) to 18 target blocks (65 components), following multi-hop paths too, at this model revision. The relationships it reads are 325 declared, 591 extracted. Extracted relationships are static imports only: they say which files name which, never that the code runs that way.
- Explanation: No path of derived interactions of kinds depends-on, imports, invokes, composes, notifies, exercises, subscribes, reads, writes, submits-edits leads from the 1 source block to the 18 target blocks at cut "l1". Absence transfers exactly: no chain of relationships leads from any of the 98 source components to any of the 65 target components. However, the assumption "extracted-facts-current" is recorded as refuted (the import scan found 14 file-system imports with no declared relationship: src/core/derivation-cache.js (derivation-cache) imports node:fs (filesystem); src/core/lean.js (lean-call-boundary) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/build-engine.js (rust-engine-oracle) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/check-spec.js (spec-checker) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/conformance.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/differential.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/gen-engine-inputs.js (formal-semantics) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/make-benchmark.js (benchmark-generator) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/prove-integrity.js (integrity-rules) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/prove.js (proof-export) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/record-goldens.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/rust-engine.js (rust-engine-oracle) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/spec-store.js (spec-store) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js (dev-server-plugin) imports node:fs (filesystem)), so the claim is undecided: it may still hold, but what it rests on does not.

**Holds only if**

- `static-extractor-sound`: The static extractor (tools/extract.js) reports exactly the static imports of the files it reads: every import, re-export and dynamic import of a JavaScript file, every import line of a Lean file, every mod and use of a Rust file, resolved to the file it names, and nothing else.
- `extracted-facts-current`: The fact file of extracted imports (specs/<project>/extracted/facts.json) describes the files on disk: the digest of every file of the implementation surface equals the digest recorded in it, by the extractor version that wrote it.

**Counterexample**

None.

## edit-representations-equivalent

After a valid edit, at quiescence the scene (projection and renderer), the inspector and the source editor all show the revision of the transaction system, for a visual edit and for a source edit.

- Kind: temporal
- Method: eventually
- **Status**: Verified within the stated model and assumptions
- Check: single-trace: Decided by running 2 hand-written scenarios ("visual-edit-flow", "source-edit-flow"), with no exhaustive exploration and no refinement: a check of these traces, not of all executions.
- Grounding: self-contained: The behaviors the check reads ("renderer-flow", "transaction-flow", "projection-flow", "inspector-flow", "source-editor-flow") refine nothing, are stored in no record, take no contract's events and read no anchored behavior; they decide on payload fields the scenarios supply (event "projection-refreshed" field "revision", event "operation-submitted" field "text", event "refresh-requested" field "revision", event "model-published" field "revision", event "source-apply-requested" field "text").
- Evaluator: exegra.trace/0.1
- Scope: Under fault model Local workstation (5 failure modes; bounds: at most 1 failures at once). Checked the final configuration of the traces of scenarios visual-edit-flow (11 steps), source-edit-flow (11 steps). This is a check of these traces, not of all possible executions.
- Explanation: The expected occurrence or final state was observed in each of the 2 traces of "visual-edit-flow" and "source-edit-flow". Each of its 2 negatives violated it: "visual-edit-flow" with publish-lost; "source-edit-flow" with publish-lost. Other executions, faults or inputs were not examined. The result holds under fault model "local-workstation" only as far as the listed scenarios inject its failure modes: they inject 0 of its 5; the rest were not exercised.

**Holds only if**

- `machines-abstract-the-code`: The scenario state machines in this file faithfully abstract the code they describe: session.apply, Project.apply and its commit, the session's onCommit and flush, the renderer, inspector and source editor refresh and the workspace write.
- `views-derive-from-committed-model`: Every representation (scene, inspector, source editor) derives its content from the committed model snapshot and from interaction state, and from nothing else.
- `tests-exercise-shipped-modules`: The core tests import and run the same modules under src/core and tools that the application and the agent command line run.
- `browser-tests-run-real-application`: The browser tests drive the real application served by the Vite development server, against scratch copies of this specification and of the multiplayer test fixture (one pair per Playwright worker) (and, read-only, a project linked locally), in the locally installed Chrome.
- `recorded-results-reflect-current-code`: A recorded result of a test in evidence/results.json was produced by the code and the specification as they are now.

**Counterexample**

None.

## core-logic-in-lean

Every implemented component of the verified kernel (facet verdict-kernel, the components tagged verdict-kernel: the code that decides whether a claim holds, namely model derivation, the semantic hash, engine-input derivation, the engine, the explorer and temporal checker, claim verdicts and their helpers, and transactions) is implemented in Lean, so the claims about the kernel are theorems about the code that runs. Everything else (layout, projections, scene, generators, UI, live replay, request-log rendering, command-line arguments, workspace I/O, the JSONC text layer) is JavaScript outside the verdict path.

- Kind: structural
- Method: implementation-language
- **Status**: Inconclusive
- Check: structural: Decided over the whole declared component graph by "implementation-language".
- Grounding: bound: Reads the declared components and relationships of the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.structural/0.1
- Scope: Checked the declared implementation.language of 17 implemented components at this model revision. The declaration is not compared with the files (the assumption that declarations match the code).
- Explanation: 7 of 17 implemented components are lean; 10 are not yet (js: expression-language, queries, transactions, claim-evaluator, contract-conformance, data-model, metric-claims, fault-model; mixed: simulation-contract, workload-model). Porting the rest is planned (WG-4785). However, the assumption "extracted-facts-current" is recorded as refuted (the import scan found 14 file-system imports with no declared relationship: src/core/derivation-cache.js (derivation-cache) imports node:fs (filesystem); src/core/lean.js (lean-call-boundary) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/build-engine.js (rust-engine-oracle) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/check-spec.js (spec-checker) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/conformance.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/differential.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/gen-engine-inputs.js (formal-semantics) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/make-benchmark.js (benchmark-generator) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/prove-integrity.js (integrity-rules) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/prove.js (proof-export) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/record-goldens.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/rust-engine.js (rust-engine-oracle) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/spec-store.js (spec-store) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js (dev-server-plugin) imports node:fs (filesystem)), so the claim is undecided: it may still hold, but what it rests on does not.

**Holds only if**

- `static-extractor-sound`: The static extractor (tools/extract.js) reports exactly the static imports of the files it reads: every import, re-export and dynamic import of a JavaScript file, every import line of a Lean file, every mod and use of a Rust file, resolved to the file it names, and nothing else.
- `extracted-facts-current`: The fact file of extracted imports (specs/<project>/extracted/facts.json) describes the files on disk: the digest of every file of the implementation surface equals the digest recorded in it, by the extractor version that wrote it.

**Counterexample**

- Summary: Not yet lean: expression-language, queries, transactions, claim-evaluator, contract-conformance, data-model, metric-claims, simulation-contract, fault-model, workload-model.
- Entities: `expression-language`, `queries`, `transactions`, `claim-evaluator`, `contract-conformance`, `data-model`, `metric-claims`, `simulation-contract`, `fault-model`, `workload-model`

## model-references-resolve

In a derived model whose diagnostics contain no reference diagnostic (ref.unresolved, ref.kind), every reference names an entity of the model whose kind the reference allows.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: lean-theorem
- **Status**: Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code
- Check: lean-theorem: A theorem of the Lean core, "Exegra.Model.derive_refs_resolve", checked by the Lean kernel; it covers every input.
- Grounding: bound: States a property of the Lean definitions "Exegra.Model.derive".
- Evaluator: lean.kernel/4.35.0
- Scope: Checked by Lean 4.35.0 at 2026-11-28T13:20:18.726Z, as recorded: the theorem's proof re-checked by the kernel and its axioms collected. It establishes the statement about the Lean definitions it names. That the code which runs is those definitions rests on the trusted base: the Lean compiler and runtime, the WebAssembly toolchain and the JS glue (the claim's assumptions).
- Explanation: "Exegra.Model.derive_refs_resolve" is a theorem; the Lean kernel accepted its proof again; it depends only on propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound; its statement is about Exegra.Model.derive (statement hash 69112d7ba70f).

**Holds only if**

- `trusted-lean-kernel`: The Lean 4 kernel of the toolchain formal/lean-toolchain pins (v4.35.0) accepts only well-typed proof terms.
- `trusted-lean-compiler-runtime`: The Lean compiler translates the Lean core faithfully to C, and the Lean v4.35.0 C/C++ runtime, with the two-hunk patch formal/wasm/patches/lean-v4.35.0-runtime-wasm.patch applied, executes that C as Lean defines it.
- `trusted-emscripten-toolchain`: Emscripten 7.0.0 (clang/LLVM, wasm-ld, binaryen, musl libc, libc++ and the generated JS loader) compiles and links the C of the Lean core and the runtime to WebAssembly (wasm64, Memory64) without changing its meaning.
- `trusted-libuv`: libuv v1.48.0, linked into the WebAssembly module because the Lean runtime references it, does not affect the result of a call: the core reaches none of its I/O.
- `trusted-js-glue`: The glue between JavaScript and the Lean core (src/core/lean.js, formal/wasm/glue.c, the emscripten-generated exegra-lean.mjs) passes request and reply bytes unchanged and reports failures as errors.
- `trusted-wasm-engine`: The WebAssembly engine of the host (V8 in Node and Chrome, SpiderMonkey in Firefox) executes the module according to the WebAssembly specification, Memory64 and exception handling included.

**Counterexample**

None.

**Evidence**

None is attached.

None.

**Evidence**

None is attached.

## model-derivation-matches-javascript

The Lean model derivation and semantic hash give the diagnostics, model and hash of the JavaScript parser they replaced on every specification the tests read (specs/exegra, specs/benchmark, tests/fixtures) and on every specification text the core tests parse, the invalid ones included.

- Kind: structural
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1
- Scope: No model evaluation was performed. This rests on 3 evidence entities (ev-model-kernel-corpus, ev-model-kernel-cases, ev-model-kernel-edges), of which 3 have a recorded execution result; the rest is author-entered.
- Explanation: Exegra holds a recorded result of executing ev-model-kernel-corpus, ev-model-kernel-cases and ev-model-kernel-edges: pass.

**Holds only if**

- `trusted-lean-kernel`: The Lean 4 kernel of the toolchain formal/lean-toolchain pins (v4.35.0) accepts only well-typed proof terms.
- `trusted-lean-compiler-runtime`: The Lean compiler translates the Lean core faithfully to C, and the Lean v4.35.0 C/C++ runtime, with the two-hunk patch formal/wasm/patches/lean-v4.35.0-runtime-wasm.patch applied, executes that C as Lean defines it.
- `trusted-emscripten-toolchain`: Emscripten 7.0.0 (clang/LLVM, wasm-ld, binaryen, musl libc, libc++ and the generated JS loader) compiles and links the C of the Lean core and the runtime to WebAssembly (wasm64, Memory64) without changing its meaning.
- `trusted-libuv`: libuv v1.48.0, linked into the WebAssembly module because the Lean runtime references it, does not affect the result of a call: the core reaches none of its I/O.
- `trusted-js-glue`: The glue between JavaScript and the Lean core (src/core/lean.js, formal/wasm/glue.c, the emscripten-generated exegra-lean.mjs) passes request and reply bytes unchanged and reports failures as errors.
- `trusted-wasm-engine`: The WebAssembly engine of the host (V8 in Node and Chrome, SpiderMonkey in Firefox) executes the module according to the WebAssembly specification, Memory64 and exception handling included.

**Counterexample**

None.

## product-layers-checked

A project's product files (project.jsonc "products") are validated together with the platform specification: a product that imports the platform and declares only the allowed sections passes, and each of these is refused with its own named rule, whose message names the offending id or section and says how to fix it: an imported id the platform does not declare (layer.import-unknown), a redefinition of a platform entity or of another product's (layer.redefines-entity), a section a product may not declare (layer.kind-not-allowed), a platform entity used without being imported (layer.import-missing), a reference nothing declares (layer.reference-unknown), a missing header (layer.header-missing), an import of another platform file (layer.import-mismatch) a reference into another product (layer.cross-product-reference), two product files with one product id (layer.product-id-duplicate), an entity id that fails the id pattern or is missing (layer.entity-id-invalid, layer.entity-id-missing) and a platform entity that names a product entity (layer.platform-references-product). A platform entity named under any reference key of the meta-model requires an import. Transactions edit only the platform text and refuse a product's entities (layer.product-entity).

- Kind: structural
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1
- Scope: No model evaluation was performed. - Explanation: Exegra holds a recorded result of executing ev-layers-accepted, ev-layers-cli, ev-layers-import-unknown, ev-layers-redefines, ev-layers-redefines-product, ev-layers-kind, ev-layers-import-missing, ev-layers-reference-unknown, ev-layers-header-missing, ev-layers-import-mismatch, ev-layers-cross-product, ev-layers-transactions, ev-layers-product-id-duplicate, ev-layers-reference-keys, ev-layers-platform-references-product, ev-layers-reload and ev-layers-header-version-platform: pass.

## product-layers-no-platform-power

A product file cannot claim what only the platform may have: a role at platform level or with anonymous, service or external holders, a contract that is public with the anonymous scheme, is implemented by a platform component or carries a realisation of generated, hand or derived, a behavior at implementation level, an event that is a domain event, an elapsed-time event or states a delivery guarantee, a capability that is core and a setting that is set at or locked by the platform level are each refused with layer.platform-power, naming the entity and the fix. specc refuses the same product files with the same rule codes.

## product-layers-typed-sections

A product file may declare the typed sections objects, policies, queries, views, workflows, segments, settings, ledgerUnits, ledgerPools, ledgerLimits, mailTemplates, noticeTemplates, tokens, triggers, automations and operations, each value of which is checked against the platform type its section maps to (object-type-def, policy-def, query-def, view-def, workflow-def, segment-decl, setting-def, ledger-unit-decl, ledger-pool-decl, ledger-limit, mail-template-decl, notice-template-decl, token-decl, trigger-def, automation-def, tenant-operation-def): a value that does not have the shape of its type is refused with layer.value-invalid, a union value the platform union lacks with layer.outside-catalogue, and a name in the product namespace that no product entity declares with layer.reference-unknown or that another product declares with layer.cross-product-reference. The typed sections are not entities of the combined model and do not change the platform semantic hash. specc reads the same table and gives the same rule codes.

## product-layers-namespace

Every entity id of a product file begins with the namespace of its product, the product id with each hyphen replaced by an underscore followed by two underscores, and a local name; an id outside it is refused with layer.namespace-missing. A capability a product declares is confined to that product: another product file that names it is refused with layer.cross-product-reference, and a product capability is reported unreachable only when no role or grant confers it.

## product-layers-expressions

An expression written as text in a typed section of a product file (a policy condition, a trigger condition, an operation's check, a field default or read rule, a constraint, a segment predicate, an automation's expression) is parsed and checked with the tenant rule language in the scope it is written in: a text that does not parse, or uses a name or call the language lacks, is refused with layer.expr-syntax; a record field the object type of the scope lacks with layer.expr-unknown-field; a role neither the platform nor the product declares with layer.expr-unknown-role; a param the scope does not bind with layer.expr-unknown-param; and an operand of a shape its operator does not accept, a comparison of two shapes, or a hop through a field that is not a single reference with layer.expr-type. The nine operators of the language (checked + - *, a timestamp plus or minus milliseconds, ms_between, days, hours, minutes, concat, text) are parsed and typed as the language declares them, with the sign rule for a - before a digit. Each refusal names the product entity, the place and text, and the position in the text. specc gives the same rule codes.

- Kind: structural
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Inconclusive
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1

- Explanation: Exegra holds a recorded result of executing ev-layers-expressions-scope, ev-layers-expressions-check, ev-layers-expressions-syntax, ev-layers-expressions-types, ev-layers-expressions-refused, ev-layers-expressions-free, ev-layers-expressions-operators, ev-layers-expressions-operator-types, ev-layers-expressions-sign, ev-layers-expressions-precedence, ev-layers-expressions-time-time, ev-layers-expressions-step-params, ev-layers-expressions-drift, ev-layers-expressions-drift-detects and ev-layers-expressions-corpus: pass. ## derivation-cache-keyed-by-content

A cached derivation is reused only for the same input contents and the same Exegra derivation sources: changing a specification file, the file the project config names or any source under src/core or src/engine/lean is a miss, and a missing, truncated or altered entry is a miss that is replaced, never an error.

- Method: external-evidence

- Explanation: Exegra holds a recorded result of executing ev-derivation-cache-inputs, ev-derivation-cache-sources and ev-derivation-cache-damaged: pass.

## product-layers-share-platform-analysis

Validating several sets of product files over one platform text in one process parses the platform text once, and every validation reports exactly what a validation of that set alone reports: the same errors, warnings, gaps, semantic hash and layered hash. The shared platform analysis is read-only, so no validation can alter what the next one sees.

## core-static-dependencies-acyclic

The static dependencies (relationship kinds of class static: depends-on, imports) among the components of the semantic core form no cycle.

- Kind: structural
- Method: acyclic
- **Status**: Inconclusive
- Check: structural: Decided over the whole declared component graph by "acyclic".
- Grounding: bound: The relationships it reads are declared by authors (28 declared); nothing checked them against the code.
- Evaluator: exegra.structural/0.1
- Scope: Checked the 233 relationships of all kinds of class static restricted to 49 components for directed cycles at this model revision. The relationships it reads are 28 declared, 591 extracted. Extracted relationships are static imports only: they say which files name which, never that the code runs that way.
- Explanation: The relationships "extracted:imports:formal/Exegra/Format2B.lean=>formal/Exegra/Elab.lean", "extracted:imports:formal/Exegra/Core/Behav.lean=>formal/Exegra/Resource.lean" and "extracted:imports:formal/Exegra/Format2.lean=>formal/Exegra/Expr.lean" form a directed cycle: formal-semantics -> extracted:imports:formal/Exegra/Format2B.lean=>formal/Exegra/Elab.lean -> lts-foundation -> extracted:imports:formal/Exegra/Core/Behav.lean=>formal/Exegra/Resource.lean -> resource-model -> extracted:imports:formal/Exegra/Format2.lean=>formal/Exegra/Expr.lean -> formal-semantics. However, the assumption "extracted-facts-current" is recorded as refuted (the import scan found 14 file-system imports with no declared relationship: src/core/derivation-cache.js (derivation-cache) imports node:fs (filesystem); src/core/lean.js (lean-call-boundary) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/build-engine.js (rust-engine-oracle) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/check-spec.js (spec-checker) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/conformance.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/differential.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/gen-engine-inputs.js (formal-semantics) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/make-benchmark.js (benchmark-generator) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/prove-integrity.js (integrity-rules) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/prove.js (proof-export) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/record-goldens.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/rust-engine.js (rust-engine-oracle) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/spec-store.js (spec-store) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js (dev-server-plugin) imports node:fs (filesystem)), so the claim is undecided: it may still hold, but what it rests on does not.

**Holds only if**

- `static-extractor-sound`: The static extractor (tools/extract.js) reports exactly the static imports of the files it reads: every import, re-export and dynamic import of a JavaScript file, every import line of a Lean file, every mod and use of a Rust file, resolved to the file it names, and nothing else.
- `extracted-facts-current`: The fact file of extracted imports (specs/<project>/extracted/facts.json) describes the files on disk: the digest of every file of the implementation surface equals the digest recorded in it, by the extractor version that wrote it.

**Counterexample**

- Summary: Cycle formal-semantics -> extracted:imports:formal/Exegra/Format2B.lean=>formal/Exegra/Elab.lean -> lts-foundation -> extracted:imports:formal/Exegra/Core/Behav.lean=>formal/Exegra/Resource.lean -> resource-model -> extracted:imports:formal/Exegra/Format2.lean=>formal/Exegra/Expr.lean -> formal-semantics.
- Entities: `formal-semantics`, `extracted:imports:formal/Exegra/Format2B.lean=>formal/Exegra/Elab.lean`, `lts-foundation`, `extracted:imports:formal/Exegra/Core/Behav.lean=>formal/Exegra/Resource.lean`, `resource-model`, `extracted:imports:formal/Exegra/Format2.lean=>formal/Exegra/Expr.lean`
- Path: formal-semantics -> extracted:imports:formal/Exegra/Format2B.lean=>formal/Exegra/Elab.lean -> lts-foundation -> extracted:imports:formal/Exegra/Core/Behav.lean=>formal/Exegra/Resource.lean -> resource-model -> extracted:imports:formal/Exegra/Format2.lean=>formal/Exegra/Expr.lean -> formal-semantics

**Evidence**

None is attached.

## visual-edit-views-not-ahead-of-commit

In every reachable configuration of a valid visual edit, under every fault mask of the scenario and every order of simultaneous events, the projection never shows a revision later than the committed one, and the file holds either the text it started with or the committed text, never a text not yet committed.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: state-invariant
- **Status**: Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code
- Check: single-trace: Decided by running one hand-written scenario ("visual-edit-flow"), with no exhaustive exploration and no refinement: a check of these traces, not of all executions.
- Grounding: self-contained: The behaviors the check reads ("projection-flow", "transaction-flow", "workspace-flow") refine nothing, are stored in no record, take no contract's events and read no anchored behavior; they decide on payload fields the scenarios supply (event "refresh-requested" field "revision", event "operation-submitted" field "text", event "write-intent" field "file", event "write-intent" field "baseHash", event "write-intent" field "text", event "write-intent" field "requestId").
- Evaluator: exegra.trace/0.1
- Scope: Checked every configuration of the traces of scenarios visual-edit-flow (11 steps). This is a check of these traces, not of all possible executions.
- Explanation: The expression held in the initial configuration and after every step of each of the 1 trace of "visual-edit-flow". Each of its 2 negatives violated it: "visual-edit-flow" with refresh-before-commit at step 1; "visual-edit-flow" with save-before-refresh at step 1. Other executions, faults or inputs were not examined.

**Holds only if**

- `views-derive-from-committed-model`: Every representation (scene, inspector, source editor) derives its content from the committed model snapshot and from interaction state, and from nothing else.

## decomposition-behavioural-validity

A candidate decomposition whose blocks, read as the composites of a cut, carry a message that no relationship between the two elements witnesses (V5), a declared abstraction that does not refine its members (V6), or a contract on a boundary between two blocks that the events crossing it do not type (V7) is rejected with the failing condition and its witnesses; where no block declares abstract behaviour V6 is reported as not applicable, with the reason, and not as a pass; a hop between a block and the outside of the scope is the scope's boundary and no candidate's (M7 criterion 3).

## decomposition-acceptance-transaction

Accepting a valid candidate is one transaction of the project that records the components its blocks need, a named hierarchy and two cuts of it; undo reverses it as one step; a candidate that is not valid, or whose blocks cannot be recorded, is refused and changes nothing; the application navigates the recorded hierarchy at once and a level-bound structural claim evaluates at its cuts (M7 criterion 6, invariants I4 and I5).

- Method: external-evidence

## metric-claims-report-basis

Every metric-bound claim result reports its runs, seeds, passes, N', fraction, confidence, the spread of the per-run statistic and its underpowered and truncated runs; a positive result is model-holds, never verified and never supported; samples follow the request lifecycle; an underpowered run (a quantile, mean, or ratio of counts with fewer than 30 expected on its rarer side) is left out only for an open-started class and otherwise does not satisfy the bound; it reads model-holds or fails only when the exact binomial test on the N' runs kept decides it and inconclusive otherwise; and a negative that does not fail, or a control negative that does not hold, makes it inconclusive.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Not evaluated
- Check: evidence: The status comes from attached evidence that is not a test.

## lean-theorem-records-stale-on-source-change

Each recorded Lean theorem check carries the hash of the formal/ sources it was checked on, the records stamp of the tree and the hash of the theorem statement as Lean printed it; when Lean does not run (not installed, or EXEGRA_NO_LEAN=1) a recorded check whose formal hash differs from that of the current sources, or that carries none, does not keep its claim proved: the claim is not evaluated, with an explanation that starts with Stale and the diagnostic claim.stale-theorem.

## concordance-results-are-evidence

A linked project's Concordance results (concordanceResults in project.jsonc) become derived test evidence of their claims: a pass makes a requirement supported and never verified, a fail makes it violated, a claim without an entry stays not evaluated, and a results file for another tool or specification, or an entry for an unknown claim, changes no status. A results file that names a specSha256 (the sha-256 of the specification text it was computed from) is not applied when that differs from the current text; one that names none is applied with its revision recorded as unknown, and its explanation says so.

## refines-checks-machine-refinement

A refines claim is verified only when, in the traces of the scenarios that run the implementation behavior and in every execution of their inputs that delivers simultaneous entries in another order, every initial configuration and every fired transition of it maps through its refinement mapping to the step its model machine takes or to a stutter, every input it ignores is one its model ignores from the mapped configuration, and at least one step matched a model transition; a step to the wrong model state, a step whose model guard is false, a wrong mapped post-variable, a moving step mapped to a stutter, different emitted events, a wrong initial state and an ignored input the model would take (reason ignored-input) each make it violated, with the failing step as the last step of the counterexample trace. Timers the implementation sets are compared with the model's timers when the model transition sets some and are internal to the implementation otherwise; a model guard or effect that reads another behavior reads it from the same trace, through the machine that refines that behavior when it does not run itself. With refinement.timeScale, the model reads the trace time divided by it and its timer delays are compared multiplied by it. A delayed emit of the model is realised only by a delayed emit of the implementation for the same event and the payload fields the model sets, delivered after the model delay times the time scale; a timer never realises an emit, and an undelayed emit for a delayed one, or the reverse, is violated (reason emits). A failure the engine's order does not reach is violated with the delivery order in the counterexample; an exploration that reaches its configuration cap or the step limit, or a scenario with faults, sampled delays, cancellations or instances, is inconclusive and never verified.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1
- Scope: No model evaluation was performed. This rests on 26 evidence entities (ev-refines-verified, ev-refines-wrong-state, ev-refines-guard-false, ev-refines-post-variable, ev-refines-false-stutter, ev-refines-initial, ev-refines-emits, ev-refines-declaration-order, ev-refines-timer-same, ev-refines-timer-internal, ev-refines-timer-differs, ev-refines-cross-refined, ev-refines-cross-direct, ev-refines-cross-violated, ev-refines-time-scale, ev-refines-time-scale-violated, ev-refines-time-scale-absent, ev-refines-delayed-emit-scaled, ev-refines-delayed-emit-event, ev-refines-delayed-emit-delay, ev-refines-delayed-emit-at-once, ev-refines-timer-not-emit, ev-refines-ignored-input, ev-refines-other-order, ev-refines-explore-step-limit, ev-refines-explore-cap), of which 26 have a recorded execution result; the rest is author-entered.
- Explanation: Exegra holds a recorded result of executing ev-refines-verified, ev-refines-wrong-state, ev-refines-guard-false, ev-refines-post-variable, ev-refines-false-stutter, ev-refines-initial, ev-refines-emits, ev-refines-declaration-order, ev-refines-timer-same, ev-refines-timer-internal, ev-refines-timer-differs, ev-refines-cross-refined, ev-refines-cross-direct, ev-refines-cross-violated, ev-refines-time-scale, ev-refines-time-scale-violated, ev-refines-time-scale-absent, ev-refines-delayed-emit-scaled, ev-refines-delayed-emit-event, ev-refines-delayed-emit-delay, ev-refines-delayed-emit-at-once, ev-refines-timer-not-emit, ev-refines-ignored-input, ev-refines-other-order, ev-refines-explore-step-limit and ev-refines-explore-cap: pass.

## machine-transition-coverage

For every implementation-level machine whose realisation is shipped, exegra cover-machines reports, per machine, the transitions and guard outcomes (true and false of each guard) covered out of the total, read from the traces of the scenarios that run it; for each item they leave out it searches bounded sequences of the machine's input events with representative payloads and waits, keeps the runs that reach it as generated scenarios cover-<machine>-<n> whose traces exercise it, and writes them with --write as CreateEntity operations tagged generated-coverage that apply accepts and that, once applied, cover the same items; a transition reached by no scenario and no searched sequence is machine.transition-unreachable, a warning, or an error under system.generation.

## crash-instance-loses-state

A crash-instance fault stops exactly the named instance at its time: its incarnation is incremented, its timers removed, its waiting requests removed with their timeouts, its in-service requests ended with their service-done entries and its units released, its restart drawn, its notify event broadcast; every targeted entry created for the old incarnation, a reply to a call it made before the crash included, is dropped as stale; a routed entry decided for it is ignored by that behavior only, and a broadcast skips it; instance aggregates exclude it while it is down; round-robin, random, any and least-busy routes skip it; a restart edge restores only its own incarnation; a crash of a missing or already down instance does nothing; and on restart the instance is in its initial state with its initial variables.

## prove-reports-only-lean-accepted

exegra prove reports a claim result only when Lean accepted its theorem.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: state-invariant
- **Status**: Verified within the stated model and assumptions
- Check: single-trace: Decided by running 2 hand-written scenarios ("prove-accepted", "prove-lean-rejects"), with no exhaustive exploration and no refinement: a check of these traces, not of all executions.
- Grounding: self-contained: The behaviors the check reads ("prove-flow") refine nothing, are stored in no record, take no contract's events and read no anchored behavior; they decide on payload fields the scenarios supply (event "lean-check-finished" field "accepted").
- Evaluator: exegra.trace/0.1
- Scope: Under fault model Local workstation (5 failure modes; bounds: at most 1 failures at once). Checked every configuration of the traces of scenarios prove-accepted (9 steps), prove-lean-rejects (10 steps). This is a check of these traces, not of all possible executions.
- Explanation: The expression held in the initial configuration and after every step of each of the 2 traces of "prove-accepted" and "prove-lean-rejects". Each of its 1 negative violated it: "prove-lean-rejects" with prove-reports-unchecked at step 8. Other executions, faults or inputs were not examined. The result holds under fault model "local-workstation" only as far as the listed scenarios inject its failure modes: they inject 0 of its 5; the rest were not exercised.

**Holds only if**

- `machines-abstract-the-code`: The scenario state machines in this file faithfully abstract the code they describe: session.apply, Project.apply and its commit, the session's onCommit and flush, the renderer, inspector and source editor refresh and the workspace write.

None.

**Evidence**

None is attached.

## engine-run-terminates-proved

Every run of an engine input that the engine elaborates into the behaviour calculus stops: its outcome is never running, and it has at most stepLimit steps.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: lean-theorem
- **Status**: Proved in Lean: a kernel-checked theorem about the code
- Check: lean-theorem: A theorem of the Lean core, "Exegra.V2.runB_terminates", checked by the Lean kernel; it covers every input.
- Grounding: bound: States a property of the Lean definitions "Exegra.V2.runInput".
- Evaluator: lean.kernel/4.35.0
- Scope: Checked by Lean 4.35.0 at 2026-11-28T13:20:18.726Z, as recorded: the theorem's proof re-checked by the kernel and its axioms collected. It establishes the statement about the Lean definitions it names. That the code which runs is those definitions rests on the trusted base: the Lean compiler and runtime, the WebAssembly toolchain and the JS glue (the claim's assumptions).
- Explanation: "Exegra.V2.runB_terminates" is a theorem; the Lean kernel accepted its proof again; it depends only on propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound; its statement is about Exegra.V2.runInput (statement hash d537f0b8db90).

**Holds only if**

- `trusted-lean-kernel`: The Lean 4 kernel of the toolchain formal/lean-toolchain pins (v4.35.0) accepts only well-typed proof terms.
- `trusted-lean-compiler-runtime`: The Lean compiler translates the Lean core faithfully to C, and the Lean v4.35.0 C/C++ runtime, with the two-hunk patch formal/wasm/patches/lean-v4.35.0-runtime-wasm.patch applied, executes that C as Lean defines it.
- `trusted-emscripten-toolchain`: Emscripten 7.0.0 (clang/LLVM, wasm-ld, binaryen, musl libc, libc++ and the generated JS loader) compiles and links the C of the Lean core and the runtime to WebAssembly (wasm64, Memory64) without changing its meaning.
- `trusted-libuv`: libuv v1.48.0, linked into the WebAssembly module because the Lean runtime references it, does not affect the result of a call: the core reaches none of its I/O.
- `trusted-js-glue`: The glue between JavaScript and the Lean core (src/core/lean.js, formal/wasm/glue.c, the emscripten-generated exegra-lean.mjs) passes request and reply bytes unchanged and reports failures as errors.
- `trusted-wasm-engine`: The WebAssembly engine of the host (V8 in Node and Chrome, SpiderMonkey in Firefox) executes the module according to the WebAssembly specification, Memory64 and exception handling included.

**Counterexample**

None.

**Evidence**

None is attached.

It establishes the statement about the Lean definitions it names. That the code which runs is those definitions rests on the trusted base: the Lean compiler and runtime, the WebAssembly toolchain and the JS glue (the claim's assumptions).

## behav-delivery-order-independent

In the behaviour calculus, under every fault mask, firing the winners a step computes in any order (any permutation, chosen per configuration and delivered entry) leads to the same successor state as the step itself: the same configuration, with time, machines, pending list and attempt counts equal (not only up to a permutation), or the error state in both orders. Emitted entries enter the pending list in machine-index order, by time, after the pending entries of the timers the winners set or cancelled are removed.

## explorer-incomplete-says-why

An exploration whose frontier is not empty is incomplete, never holds, and says why: the bound (a run hit it) or the state budget (a run was truncated). A counter from 0 to 100 with invariant n <= 50, explored to bound 20, is incomplete for its bound.

## numbers-are-exact-rationals

Every number of the Lean core is an exact rational: Value has no Float constructor and no Float occurs in formal/Exegra; a JSON number is read as the exact value of its decimal text; a version-2 input holding a decimal literal elaborates (there is no notInteger refusal) and runs on the behaviour calculus with exact arithmetic, so 0.1 + 0.2 == 0.3 evaluates to true; and fixture/expression-showcase, with inexact := 0.1 + 0.2 and quo := a / b, is accepted and records inexact 0.3 and quo 3.5.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: external-evidence

## construct-consistency-rules

The cross-cutting constructs are checked as errors whether or not the specification sets system.generation: every policy, quota, retry, audit and validation names exactly one target; a validation names a declared input or field, carries exactly the parameters of its rule, fits the class of its target, and has a valid pattern and a non-empty range; a retention states exactly one of keepFor and until, a duration, fields only and always when it anonymises, declared fields, archiveTo only when it archives, and at most one per record; a quota has a positive limit, a duration window, and a declared contract error exactly when it refuses calls of a contract; a retry has positive attempts, durations, a cap only on an exponential backoff and not below the base, and a declared idempotency key; an audit captures declared fields once each, a chain field of bytes or string exactly when chained by hash; every condition, custom rule and capture parses, reads only what is in scope and types against the declared fields; and only a requirement has realisedBy.

## ui-consistency-rules

The user interface constructs are checked as errors whether or not the specification sets system.generation: a screen route is "/" or literal and :param segments with no parameter twice, no two screens answer the same route, auth is none, session or role:<r>, and every required input of the view-model contract is a route or query parameter; a form field fills a declared input of its contract with a control that fits its class, no input is filled twice, every required input is filled by a field or an argument, and only a select has options; an action gives an argument for every required input of its contract and none for an undeclared one; arguments and navigation parameters parse, read only route parameters, view-model outputs, the principal and (for navigation) the response, and are of the right class; a navigation gives exactly the target route parameters; accessible names are non-empty; a live binding refreshes the whole view model or a path into it, once per screen and event; and every journey step has exactly one known key with nothing else in its value (no CSS selector), names existing screens, forms, fields, events and browsers, gives every route parameter on goto, selects by a WAI-ARIA role, expects an HTTP status from 100 to 599 and scans logs with regular expressions, and a journey verifies requirements only.

## wire-naming-and-json

system.generation.wireCase and a contract's binding.case accept exactly snake_case, kebab-case and camelCase, any other value being property.enum at its position; json is a built-in type that resolves wherever a type is named but is never the representation of an id or scalar (type.repr-json); in expressions a json value is its own class, compared only with == and != against json or null, refused by ordering, arithmetic, concatenation and boolean operators, by toasts and form controls, and no path reaches into it.

## ui-client-constructs-fourth-set

A contract input is encoded only as its encode allows: trim, split and as read a text input, split names a separator and is not combined with as, and empty is given only for an optional input. A form field takes an initial value only from a path reaching a value its control edits, or from the first item of a list of structs whose field equals an expression of the field’s class, and an always-derived field takes none; normalize is only on a text, textarea or password control. A form’s second call reads the first call’s response, and the outcome reads the outputs of both calls. onSuccess.view names outputs of the view model of the screen the success opens, each of its output’s class. onError prefix and announce, when given, are not empty; confirmAnnounce belongs to an action with a confirm and is not empty; alsoOn offers an action on the screens of one authentication only and reads nothing of one screen. An error message is well formed: its placeholders fit its kind, the kinds it words are errors some contract of its forms and actions lists, no two messages word the same thing for the same forms and actions, and a specification with a form or an action has a default message for refusals (a readiness gap when generation is set).

- Method: external-evidence

- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1
- Scope: No model evaluation was performed. ## telemetry-logging-rules

system.telemetry declares services with distinct ids and relative log paths (telemetry.service); an event's logging is {logged: true, service, level, name?, tenant?, fields} or {logged: false, reason} (event.logging-shape), names a declared service (event.logging-service) and a dotted lower-case log name unique within the service (event.logging-name), and logs only payload fields that exist (event.logging-field-unknown), are scalars (event.logging-field-type), are neither marked sensitive nor of a type that is sensitive through structs, lists and options (event.logging-field-sensitive), and are written under lower-case names that are not ts, level, service, event, correlation or tenant and not twice (event.logging-field-reserved); with system.generation every domain event states its logging (ready.event-logging-missing); an expectLog step names a logged event and its service (uiScenario.log-event) and expects only the line's tenant and allowlisted members (uiScenario.log-field); and a logged domain event is not reported as an unused event, since the running system emits it.

## union-map-types-rules

A type may be a tagged union (variants that are unit, struct-like or newtype, written in serde's external, internal or adjacent form, with the variant names in their own case) and a type text may be map<K, V>; validation refuses a union without variants or with a variant twice (type.union-variants), a variant field that does not resolve (type.union-field), an encoding whose tagField or contentField does not fit its tag or its variants (type.encoding), a map key that is not a string, an integer or an id or scalar over one (type.map-key), a struct that contains itself through struct fields alone (type.recursion) while allowing a cycle through a union variant, list, option or map, and a constraint (pattern, minLength, maxLength) on a type that does not take it or with an invalid regular expression (type.constraints); a union and a map value are opaque (class json) in guard expressions, and readiness (ready.*-undeclared) covers map types.

## language-construct-rules

A specification may state an expression language its users write (section languages, entity language): an AST union and a value union, shapes, scopes (record-scope with system attributes, param, constant, role-test) and operators, each with its syntax (infix, keyword-infix, prefix-word, call, literal or scope form, token, precedence, associativity), operand and result shapes, null handling and one primitive of a fixed catalogue whose semantics are pinned to the Strata kernel (crates/kernel/src/expr.rs); validation refuses two operators of one name (language.operator-duplicate), a word or symbol claimed twice (language.token-clash), a token of the wrong lexical class or a form the primitive is not written in (language.syntax), a missing or misplaced precedence or two associativities at one level (language.precedence), a primitive outside the catalogue (language.primitive-unknown), operands that disagree with the primitive arity or the AST variant (language.arity), an undeclared shape (language.shape-unknown), null handling other than the primitive's (language.nulls), an operator for a variant or case the AST does not have or a second operator for one (language.variant), and a scope that is malformed, missing for an operator or read by none (language.scope); with system.generation set an AST variant or case without an operator and an operator without a primitive are errors (ready.language-variant-uncovered, ready.language-primitive-missing).

## function-construct-rules

A specification may state pure functions (section functions, entity function): typed params, a returns type, an optional errors union, lets and a body in the function body language, which is the guard language extended with struct field access, if, let, match over unions, options, results and enums, struct and union constructors, list literals, the operations map, filter, find, any, all, fold, sortBy, take, concat, keys, values, get, insert, remove and format, eval and check of a declared language, call of another function, fail and try; validation refuses a body or let that does not parse (function.expr-syntax), a name, field, variant, type, function, language or operation that does not exist (function.expr-reference), an expression that is ill typed or a body that does not fit the return type (function.expr-type), a match that misses a case without _, has a case twice or an arm after _ (function.match-exhaustive), a call cycle through two or more functions, or a call of a function of itself on anything but a strict sub-value of a union parameter at one position shared by all its calls of itself (function.recursion), errors that are not a union or a fail or try outside that union (function.error-unknown), malformed or duplicate parameter and let names and parameter or return types that do not resolve (function.param), and an example that is malformed or does not evaluate to its result or error in Exegra's JavaScript evaluator (function.example-failed); with system.generation set a function without a success example or without an example of an error variant it can fail with is an error (ready.function-example-missing).

- Kind: invariant
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.

## set-tuple-nested-types-rules

A type text may be set<T>, a JSON array of distinct items in order, which resolves, keys maps and closes recursion as list<T> does and has the class list in guard expressions; a scenario value of a set type that repeats an item does not have the type; a union variant may be a tuple variant (tuple: true, two or more positional fields written as a JSON array in order, as serde writes {"Cmp": ["Eq", a, b]}); generic type texts nest (map<K, map<K2, V>>, list<map<K, V>>). Validation refuses a set of an unresolved type (type.field-unresolved, type.union-field), a map keyed by a set or a bad key inside a set or another map (type.map-key), a tuple variant with fewer than two fields, also marked newtype, or with an optional field (type.union-variants), and a tuple variant under an internal tag (type.encoding). A shared<T> is a sharing hint, identical to T on the wire: it resolves, keys maps, closes recursion, has the class and holds the values of T, and validation refuses a shared of an unresolved type (type.field-unresolved) and a struct that holds itself through a shared field (type.recursion).

- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1
- Scope: No model evaluation was performed. ## contract-errors-on-the-wire

A contract error states its HTTP status as status (an integer from 400 to 599, contract.error-status) or as kind (a key of system.generation.errorKinds, contract.error-kind-unknown), never both (contract.error-status-kind), and otherwise takes the default system.generation.errorKind or errorStatus; errorKinds map to statuses from 400 to 599 (generation.error-kinds) and the defaults are well formed and exclusive (generation.error-status); system.generation.errorBody names a struct type whose every field but code, message and correlation is optional (generation.error-body-type), a code field of an enum holding every kind and string message and correlation fields (generation.error-body-field, generation.error-body-code), needs errorKinds (generation.error-body-kinds) and maps exactly the six runtime failures to declared kinds (generation.error-body-runtime); when generation is set an error of a served contract with no status is ready.error-status-missing, and under an error body one with no kind is ready.error-kind-missing.

## tenant-data-fixed-rule

A record states whether it is a platform table (scope platform, its fields its whole shape) or a platform table carrying tenant-defined data (scope tenant-meta, described by the type with metamodel tenant-schema), and may be marked realisation target; once a type has metamodel tenant-schema, validation refuses a record without a scope (record.scope-missing), a record that is not tenant-meta yet names a tenant object type or carries tenant field values, through a field of an object-id or values type or a map keyed by a field-id type (record.tenant-data-fixed), and a tenant-meta record without describedBy naming the tenant-schema type, or a describedBy on any other record (record.described-by); a specification without a tenant schema is unchanged.

## route-ambiguous-rule

Two screens whose routes tie are refused with route.ambiguous: both routes are non-catch-all, have the same number of segments, agree at every position (equal literals, or a parameter on either side), and have the same number of literal segments. Screens whose routes do not tie that way are not refused.

## contract-exposure-and-auth

A contract states its exposure (public, internal or local) and its auth as {scheme, ...} with scheme anonymous, session, service, provisioning-token, signature, signed-address, delegation or in-process; with system.generation set a missing or prose auth is contract.auth-structured and a missing exposure contract.exposure, and always an object auth has only the members of its scheme (contract.auth-structured) and in-process is exactly the scheme of local contracts (contract.exposure); a local contract has no HTTP binding and an internal one none under the gateway prefix (exposure.gateway); a public contract not marked readOnly needs a session or visitor with csrf and origin or a verified credential (auth.public-write); an anonymous public contract is readOnly or justified and limited by a quota, an anonymous internal one readOnly and justified (auth.anonymous); the service scheme is never public (auth.service-public); every named policy governs the contract (auth.policy-unknown); a development contract names a declared condition, is never anonymous and when public names roles (auth.dev-only); and a public contract states a tenantScope other than platform (contract.tenant-scope).

## derivation-construct-rules

A contract may be derived (realisation derived, with derivation {kind: pure, function} | {kind: read, needs, load, decide, and optionally the need loop insufficient and needRounds together} | {kind: command, needs, load, decide, commit, conflict, insufficient, needRounds, retries}, and optionally fault) or a facade (realisation facade, with a bffRoute); validation refuses a derivation on a contract that is not derived, a derived contract without one, an unknown kind, a field of another kind or a read naming only one of insufficient and needRounds (derivation.kind), a function whose parameters are not inputs (or load outputs) of the same name and type, whose result is not the outputs, whose error variants are not errors of the contract, an insufficient variant that is not a variant of decide carrying one need, or a fault that is not an error without payload (derivation.signature), a load contract that is a facade or itself, whose required inputs nothing fills, that takes the needs not exactly once, whose outputs are named like inputs or whose errors are not errors of the contract (derivation.load), the same for the commit contract with what decide gives, a conflict that is not a commit error, and an output that is neither a commit output nor a field of what decide gives (derivation.commit), need rounds (of a command or a read with a need loop) or retries missing or not non-negative integers (derivation.bounds), and a facade with implementedBy or whose bffRoute method, path and shape not exactly one served contract states (contract.facade-route); with system.generation set a derivation naming an undeclared function is an error (ready.derived-function-missing).

## function-language-kernel-fidelity

The function body language states what the Strata kernel computes without stand-ins: an AST field typed string or an id or scalar over string (has_role's role-id) is read as a name, so has_role evaluates and checks; a variant, enum value or field named like a keyword (value.null, expr.if, then, else) is written after a dot, as a match pattern and before a colon, and a keyword-named field is never bound by shorthand; the built-in unit type has the one value () (null in examples), and unit is not the representation of an id or scalar and cannot be declared again; print(L, e) writes an expression as the kernel's Display writes it; format's {:?} writes a value as Rust's derived Debug of the type specc generates and {:json} as serde_json's compact text (strings escaped, f64 as ryu writes it); startsWith, endsWith, length, trim, chars, snakeCase, parseTime and the ASCII character classes behave as the kernel's Rust (str::starts_with, chars().count(), str::trim over White_Space, transition.rs snake_case, plain.rs parse_time, char::is_ascii_*); a function calls itself only on a strict sub-value of a union parameter; and the language evaluator and checker give the kernel's results and exact error texts on the shared case table.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1
- Scope: No model evaluation was performed. ## capability-model-closed

Capability, plan, role, grant, delegation, tokenScope and featureFlag are entity kinds, and a contract auth names the capabilities it requires in requires; every capability a contract, plan, role, grant, delegation, token scope or flag names is a capability (capability.unknown); with system.generation set every contract that is not local requires at least one (capability.required); every capability is conferred by some role or grant and is core or entitled by some plan (capability.unreachable); a delegation passes on only capabilities its source (role, token scope, grant or delegation) holds and a grant confers only capabilities its issuer role holds (delegation.exceeds); and a grant or delegation maxDuration is a positive duration (capability.duration).

- Method: external-evidence

- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1

## example-fixture-rules

A specification may declare example fixtures (section exampleFixtures, entity exampleFixture: id, type, value, description) and a function example's argument, result or error may be {fixture: id} or {fixture: id, with: {dotted path: value}} instead of the value; Exegra resolves the reference, and the overrides in order, before it evaluates the example, so the example is checked exactly as if the value were written out. Validation refuses a reference to a fixture that is not declared (example.fixture-unknown); a fixture whose type does not resolve or whose value is not of its type, and a fixture that, with its overrides, is not the type of the parameter, result or error it stands for (example.fixture-type); and an override path that does not lead into the fixture's value through struct fields, map keys, list indexes and union variant names, an empty step, or a with that is not an object (example.fixture-override).

- Kind: invariant
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1
- Scope: No model evaluation was performed. ## function-branch-coverage

Exegra records which branches of each function its examples take (both branches of if, each arm of match, the value and the error path of try where the called function can fail, and fail), without changing what the examples evaluate to; exegra cover-functions reports per function the branches covered out of the total and each uncovered branch with its place in the body or let; an uncovered branch is reported and raises no readiness gap, because a rule that asks for one example per branch rewards examples that restate the function rather than its requirement; system.generation.functionBranches is still accepted and changes nothing.

- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence

## format-gaps-rules

A contract may be a target (realisation target: specified, not implemented, served by no route, never loaded, committed or lowered onto by a derivation or facade); a derivation of a read or command may state header: true (the load with no needs first, its outputs readable by the needs function, then the needs loaded in one round that no need round counts); an untagged union (encoding tag untagged) is written as the raw JSON of its variant, read in declaration order, and validation refuses one with a tagField, contentField or variantCase, two variants written as one JSON shape or a json newtype before another variant (type.encoding); binding.suppliedBy says an input comes from the route or session and never the body, and validation refuses one naming no input or no such supplier (contract.supplied-by), and a form field or argument (form.supplied-input) or action argument (action.supplied-input) that fills one; a function body may write the JSON null where a json value is expected and nowhere else (function.expr-type), and a union variant named like a keyword (plain-json.null) after a dot of a path or in a match pattern, eval may take a lookup that following a reference reads, and sortBy orders lists item by item and unions by variant and fields, as the kernel's Value does.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1
- Scope: No model evaluation was performed. ## language-checked-arithmetic

The primitive catalogue holds checked integer arithmetic (add-checked, sub-checked, mul-checked), time arithmetic (add-checked and sub-checked on a timestamp and an int, time-difference, days-ms, hours-ms, minutes-ms) and text (concat-checked, to-text), and the language evaluator gives for them the results and exact error texts of the shared case table: every integer operand and result lies in -(2^53-1) to 2^53-1, and outside it the evaluation fails with integer-overflow (never a wrapped, saturated or rounded value), at both ends of the range and for a product beyond 64 bits; a concatenation longer than 4096 bytes of UTF-8 fails with string-too-long; a null operand gives null; an operand of the wrong kind is the error <op> does not apply to a <kind>; and the checker refuses an operand of the wrong shape (a timestamp on the right of + or -, a timestamp under *, a list under text) with <op> does not accept a <Shape> operand, giving + and - the shape of their left operand.

## language-grapheme-emoji

The primitive catalogue holds the grapheme and emoji functions (graphemes, is-emoji), and the language evaluator gives for them Strata's results over its shared case table: graphemes of a string is its count of extended grapheme clusters (UAX #29), so that a combining sequence, a ZWJ emoji sequence, a flag or a keycap is one; is-emoji of a string is true exactly when the string is one grapheme that is a keycap, a flag, a subdivision tag sequence, or ZWJ-joined parts each an emoji presentation or a text-default emoji followed by VS16 or a skin tone; a VS15 makes it not an emoji, and a lone regional indicator, a lone digit, a bare copyright, trademark or heart, two emoji and the empty string are not; a null operand gives null and an operand of another kind is an error.

## language-ancestor-of

The primitive catalogue holds ancestor-of, and the language evaluator gives for it Strata's results (WG-5510): ancestor_of(x, y, object, field) with x and y references and object and field string literals is true when x is y or one of its ancestors, the walk from y following the single reference field of the object type; it is false when the walk ends at a null field or an absent record; and it is an error, never a silent false, when the walk takes more than 32 hops, returns to a record it visited, or meets a record the lookup does not cover. Null on a null reference, an error on any other kind.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
