## edits-only-via-transactions

Following relationships that carry edits and file content (submits-edits, data-flow, writes), every path from the editing surfaces (inspector, source editor, dialogs, the composition root that turns scene drags into operations, toolbar, shell) to the specification file passes through the transaction system.

- Kind: structural
- Method: only-via
- **Status**: Inconclusive
- Check: structural: Decided over the whole declared component graph by "only-via".
- Grounding: bound: The relationships it reads are declared by authors (40 declared); nothing checked them against the code.
- Evaluator: exegra.structural/0.1
- Scope: Checked the 40 relationships of kinds submits-edits, data-flow, writes for directed paths from 6 source components to 1 target component that avoid 1 via component at this model revision.
- Explanation: A path reaches "specification-file" from "toolbar" without passing through any via component: toolbar -> toolbar-data-flow-persistence-client -> persistence-client -> persistence-client-data-flow-dev-server-plugin -> dev-server-plugin -> dev-server-plugin-data-flow-workspace-service -> workspace-service -> workspace-service-writes-specification-file -> specification-file. However, the assumption "extracted-facts-current" is recorded as refuted (the import scan found 14 file-system imports with no declared relationship: src/core/derivation-cache.js (derivation-cache) imports node:fs (filesystem); src/core/lean.js (lean-call-boundary) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/build-engine.js (rust-engine-oracle) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/check-spec.js (spec-checker) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/conformance.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/differential.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/gen-engine-inputs.js (formal-semantics) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/make-benchmark.js (benchmark-generator) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/prove-integrity.js (integrity-rules) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/prove.js (proof-export) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/record-goldens.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/rust-engine.js (rust-engine-oracle) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/spec-store.js (spec-store) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js (dev-server-plugin) imports node:fs (filesystem)), so the claim is undecided: it may still hold, but what it rests on does not.

**Holds only if**

- `static-extractor-sound`: The static extractor (tools/extract.js) reports exactly the static imports of the files it reads: every import, re-export and dynamic import of a JavaScript file, every import line of a Lean file, every mod and use of a Rust file, resolved to the file it names, and nothing else.
- `extracted-facts-current`: The fact file of extracted imports (specs/<project>/extracted/facts.json) describes the files on disk: the digest of every file of the implementation surface equals the digest recorded in it, by the extractor version that wrote it.

**Counterexample**

- Summary: Bypassing path toolbar -> toolbar-data-flow-persistence-client -> persistence-client -> persistence-client-data-flow-dev-server-plugin -> dev-server-plugin -> dev-server-plugin-data-flow-workspace-service -> workspace-service -> workspace-service-writes-specification-file -> specification-file.
- Entities: `toolbar-data-flow-persistence-client`, `persistence-client-data-flow-dev-server-plugin`, `dev-server-plugin-data-flow-workspace-service`, `workspace-service-writes-specification-file`
- Path: toolbar -> toolbar-data-flow-persistence-client -> persistence-client -> persistence-client-data-flow-dev-server-plugin -> dev-server-plugin -> dev-server-plugin-data-flow-workspace-service -> workspace-service -> workspace-service-writes-specification-file -> specification-file

**Evidence**

None is attached.

## save-follows-refresh

Every write request to the file service is preceded by a projection refresh: the write is asynchronous and happens after the views were refreshed, in all three edits that commit.

- Kind: temporal
- Method: precedes
- **Status**: Verified within the stated model and assumptions
- Check: single-trace: Decided by running 3 hand-written scenarios ("visual-edit-flow", "source-edit-flow", "edit-with-disk-conflict"), with no exhaustive exploration and no refinement: a check of these traces, not of all executions.
- Grounding: self-contained: The behaviors the check reads ("session-flow", "projection-flow", "renderer-flow", "workspace-flow") refine nothing, are stored in no record, take no contract's events and read no anchored behavior; they decide on payload fields the scenarios supply (event "transaction-committed" field "text", event "transaction-committed" field "revision", event "projection-refreshed" field "revision", event "save-succeeded" field "hash", event "refresh-requested" field "revision", event "write-intent" field "file", event "write-intent" field "baseHash", event "write-intent" field "text", event "write-intent" field "requestId").
- Evaluator: exegra.trace/0.1
- Scope: Under fault model Local workstation (5 failure modes; bounds: at most 1 failures at once). Checked the events delivered and transitions fired in the traces of scenarios visual-edit-flow (11 steps), source-edit-flow (11 steps), edit-with-disk-conflict (11 steps, faults: disk-changed-externally). This is a check of these traces, not of all possible executions. An occurrence counts as produced by the system when a transition fired or emitted it; a delivery from the scenario script does not, and an ordering between two scripted deliveries is not reported as verified.
- Explanation: Every occurrence of the "then" matcher had an earlier occurrence of the "first" matcher in each of the 3 traces of "visual-edit-flow", "source-edit-flow" and "edit-with-disk-conflict". Each of its 1 negative violated it: "visual-edit-flow" with save-before-refresh at step 1. Other executions, faults or inputs were not examined. The result holds under fault model "local-workstation" only as far as the listed scenarios inject its failure modes: they inject 1 of its 5; the rest were not exercised.

**Holds only if**

- `machines-abstract-the-code`: The scenario state machines in this file faithfully abstract the code they describe: session.apply, Project.apply and its commit, the session's onCommit and flush, the renderer, inspector and source editor refresh and the workspace write.

**Counterexample**

None.

**Evidence**

None is attached.

Declaration order decided which transition fired: "validator-reject" fired where "validator-accept" (unguarded, also enabled) is declared after it on event "model-parsed" in behavior "validator-flow". Other executions, faults or inputs were not examined. The result holds under fault model "local-workstation" only as far as the listed scenarios inject its failure modes: they inject 0 of its 5; the rest were not exercised.

## ui-expands-to-desktop

## real-runs-replay-through-self-model

The events a running Exegra really produces (requests and answers of the persistence client with the development server's own events, change notices, engine-worker messages, commits, claim evaluations, simulator controls, preference and camera storage writes) are recorded with their requestId and a logical sequence number and replayed through the behaviors of the scenario live-session; every divergence from the self-model (an event the specification does not name, an event the model does not predict at that point, an event some behavior consumes that no behavior accepts in its state, whether predicted or from outside, a payload value that differs or a predicted field the recording lacks, a predicted event not seen within 200 recorded events or by the end of a closed stream) is reported as a failed check at its step and listed in the simulator panel's Live mode, and a real open, edit and save replays without one. Only variables declared environment are calibrated from the first recorded value; any other difference is a divergence (WG-5685).

- Kind: invariant
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1
- Scope: No model evaluation was performed. This rests on 10 evidence entities (ev-replay-recorded-stream-clean, ev-replay-wrong-order, ev-replay-unknown-event, ev-replay-missing-response, ev-replay-bound, ev-replay-payload, ev-replay-not-allowed, ev-replay-incremental, ev-live-browser, ev-replay-blind-three), of which 10 have a recorded execution result; the rest is author-entered.
- Explanation: Exegra holds a recorded result of executing ev-replay-recorded-stream-clean, ev-replay-wrong-order, ev-replay-unknown-event, ev-replay-missing-response, ev-replay-bound, ev-replay-payload, ev-replay-not-allowed, ev-replay-incremental, ev-live-browser and ev-replay-blind-three: pass.

**Holds only if**

- `tests-exercise-shipped-modules`: The core tests import and run the same modules under src/core and tools that the application and the agent command line run.
- `browser-tests-run-real-application`: The browser tests drive the real application served by the Vite development server, against scratch copies of this specification and of the multiplayer test fixture (one pair per Playwright worker) (and, read-only, a project linked locally), in the locally installed Chrome.
- `recorded-results-reflect-current-code`: A recorded result of a test in evidence/results.json was produced by the code and the specification as they are now.

**Counterexample**

None.

**Evidence**

| Id | Kind | Ref | Supports | Recorded |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ev-replay-recorded-stream-clean | test | tests/core/replay.test.js#a recorded open edit and save replays through live-session without a divergence | supports | pass |
| ev-replay-wrong-order | test | tests/core/replay.test.js#an event in the wrong order is a divergence | supports | pass |
| ev-replay-unknown-event | test | tests/core/replay.test.js#an event the specification does not name is a divergence | supports | pass |
| ev-replay-missing-response | test | tests/core/replay.test.js#a predicted response that never comes is a divergence | supports | pass |
| ev-replay-bound | test | tests/core/replay.test.js#a predicted event not seen within the bound is missing even while the stream is open | supports | pass |
| ev-replay-payload | test | tests/core/replay.test.js#a recorded payload value that differs from the prediction is a divergence | supports | pass |
| ev-replay-not-allowed | test | tests/core/replay.test.js#a predicted event that no behavior consuming it accepts in its state is a divergence | supports | pass |
| ev-replay-incremental | test | tests/core/replay.test.js#the replay is incremental and deterministic | supports | pass |
| ev-live-browser | test | tests/browser/live.spec.js#live mode replays an edit and its save through the self-model on the right components without divergence | supports | pass |

## traffic-producers-name-spec-events

## browser-reloads-product-files

When a product file of the open project changes, is added or is removed on disk (or the products list of project.jsonc changes), the application's session re-derives its model from the product layers on disk: the workspace watcher reports the change as the file products, the session reads the project again, and the platform text and layout in memory are kept. A product file that does not parse keeps the last valid model.

## navigation-filters-report-removals

Nothing a view's filter or the viewer's hiding removes is silently lost: a connection through hidden blocks is drawn as a labelled derived path summary (a may-path) carrying the relationships along it, and every other removed interaction, relationship or path is counted in the scene's notes; the "N inside" badge counts only what the filter keeps. ## layout-auto-reads-multiscale

The automatic layout scores lines drawn through unrelated nodes as well as crossings, counts parallel edges between two nodes once, scores whitespace on a log scale, and scores labels that collide when the placement is fitted into a nominal viewport; interchangeable leaves take the slots that make edges leaving containers cross least, and around a hub much larger than its neighbours (an opened system) the radial method places the neighbours on rectangular rings hugging it. A hub with its neighbours is therefore not drawn as a grid with spokes through other nodes, an opened system with its environment gets most of the picture, and the layout stays deterministic and keeps pins.

- Kind: structural
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.

## camera-restored-in-model

In the camera-memory model, coming back to a view restores the camera the user left it with.

- Kind: temporal
- Method: eventually
- **Status**: Verified within the stated model and assumptions
- Check: single-trace: Decided by running one hand-written scenario ("camera-remembered"), with no exhaustive exploration and no refinement: a check of these traces, not of all executions.
- Grounding: self-contained: The behaviors the check reads ("camera-memory-flow") refine nothing, are stored in no record, take no contract's events and read no anchored behavior; they decide on payload fields the scenarios supply (event "camera-moved" field "camera", event "view-switched" field "view", event "camera-restored" field "camera", event "camera-restored" field "view", event "camera-none" field "view").
- Evaluator: exegra.trace/0.1
- Scope: Under fault model Local workstation (5 failure modes; bounds: at most 1 failures at once). Checked the final configuration of the traces of scenarios camera-remembered (12 steps). This is a check of these traces, not of all possible executions.
- Explanation: The expected occurrence or final state was observed in each of the 1 trace of "camera-remembered". Each of its 1 negative violated it: "camera-remembered" with camera-save-lost. Other executions, faults or inputs were not examined. The result holds under fault model "local-workstation" only as far as the listed scenarios inject its failure modes: they inject 0 of its 5; the rest were not exercised.

**Holds only if**

- `machines-abstract-the-code`: The scenario state machines in this file faithfully abstract the code they describe: session.apply, Project.apply and its commit, the session's onCommit and flush, the renderer, inspector and source editor refresh and the workspace write.

**Counterexample**

None.

**Evidence**

None is attached.

## agent-interface-uses-transactions

The agent command line reaches the transaction system along its relationships.

- Kind: structural
- Method: reachable
- **Status**: Inconclusive
- Check: structural: Decided over the whole declared component graph by "reachable".
- Grounding: bound: The relationships it reads are declared by authors (354 declared); nothing checked them against the code.
- Evaluator: exegra.structural/0.1
- Scope: Checked directed reachability over the 778 relationships of all kinds from 1 source component to 1 target component at this model revision. The relationships it reads are 354 declared, 591 extracted. Extracted relationships are static imports only: they say which files name which, never that the code runs that way.

However, the assumption "extracted-facts-current" is recorded as refuted (the import scan found 14 file-system imports with no declared relationship: src/core/derivation-cache.js (derivation-cache) imports node:fs (filesystem); src/core/lean.js (lean-call-boundary) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/build-engine.js (rust-engine-oracle) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/check-spec.js (spec-checker) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/conformance.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/differential.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/gen-engine-inputs.js (formal-semantics) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/make-benchmark.js (benchmark-generator) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/prove-integrity.js (integrity-rules) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/prove.js (proof-export) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/record-goldens.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/rust-engine.js (rust-engine-oracle) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/spec-store.js (spec-store) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js (dev-server-plugin) imports node:fs (filesystem)), so the claim is undecided: it may still hold, but what it rests on does not.

**Holds only if**

## persistence-confined-to-workspace

Project names are validated and every path is resolved inside specs/<project>/ before any read or write; names and paths that escape that directory are refused, and generated files can only be written under generated/. The one exception is deliberate: a project.jsonc may name a specification file outside the project directory (a linked project, such as a specification kept in its own repository), which is then read and written there. project.jsonc is not writable through the workspace endpoints (write and import accept only spec.jsonc, layout.jsonc and evidence/results.json), so only a person editing the file on disk can point a project elsewhere.

## verify-reuses-fresh-outcomes-only

A verify run takes the outcome of a test evidence entry from a recorded test run only when that run was complete, the tree did not change while it ran, and its tree stamp equals the stamp of the tree verify runs on; a reference matching no recorded test is run, never assumed to pass.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1
- Scope: No model evaluation was performed. This rests on 1 evidence entity (ev-verify-reuses-fresh-outcomes), of which 1 has a recorded execution result; the rest is author-entered.
- Explanation: Exegra holds a recorded result of executing ev-verify-reuses-fresh-outcomes: pass.

**Holds only if**

- `tests-exercise-shipped-modules`: The core tests import and run the same modules under src/core and tools that the application and the agent command line run.

**Counterexample**

None.

**Evidence**

| Id | Kind | Ref | Supports | Recorded |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ev-verify-reuses-fresh-outcomes | test | tests/core/outcome-reuse.test.js#outcomes are used only while the tree is the one they ran on | supports | pass |

## verify-skip-records-not-run

A verify run in which a test evidence reference skipped (a matched test skipped itself or is a todo, wholly or in part, or a browser test was not started under --skip-browser) records that evidence as not-run, which counts as no result, and never keeps an older pass for it; the only result kept is that of a browser test not started whose earlier result was recorded on the same tree stamp.

## admit-rejects-downgrades-and-removals

exegra admit ranks not-evaluated with inconclusive, so a claim that moves from a positive status to not-evaluated is the reason admit.claim-regressed (not acknowledgeable), and a claim that was proved, verified, supported or model-holds and that the change removes is the reason admit.claim-removed, which only an explicit --accept admit.claim-removed acknowledges.

## verify-fails-on-regression

exegra verify records, with each passing run, every claim's status and the fingerprint of each violated claim's counterexample (claimBaseline in results.json), and fails when, against that baseline, a positive claim falls to a lower status (inconclusive and not-evaluated included), a positive claim no longer exists, or a claim named by --expect-violated is violated with another counterexample; --accept-change names the claims whose change is accepted, and a failing run leaves the baseline as it was.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Supported by external evidence
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.

| Id | Kind | Ref | Supports | Recorded |

## realisation-roots-traced

When project.jsonc declares realisation roots, traceability checks each module written <alias>:<path> against that root's directory, reports a module naming an undeclared alias as missing, and reports unclaimed files only for roots that declare include globs (minus exclude globs); with no roots declared, the traceability result is the same as without the feature.

## external-not-run-distinct-from-fail

External test evidence is recorded as fail only when a matched JUnit test case reports a failure whose text matches none of the root's environmental patterns; a test absent from the report, skipped, reported as an error, or failing for an environmental reason is recorded as not-run, which `verify` never counts as a failure and the claim evaluator treats as having no result.

## cli-registers-projects-from-scripts

## specification-manifest-parts

A specification may be a manifest (specParts) listing part files, and Exegra reads it as the one document the parts make: array sections are concatenated in manifest order, a section that is not an array may be in one part only, and a missing part, a part that is itself a manifest, a manifest with sections of its own, a section two parts both hold and an id two parts give one section are refused with an error that names the files. The semantic hash is that of the one-file specification. exegra apply writes each changed entity back to the part that holds it, and a new entity to the part the manifest's specSections table names for its section (else beside its section, else the first part).

## module-to-element-map-is-a-function

Every file of the traceability surface belongs to exactly one module element, and each module element to exactly one element, from the system exegra through its subsystems and components: a file covered by two module elements is the traceability error traceability.multiple-owners, src/core/builtin-types.js belongs to generation-readiness only, and src/app/main.js is the composition-root element directly under exegra.

- Kind: structural
- Method: external-evidence
- **Status**: Inconclusive
- Check: test: The status comes from attached test evidence.
- Grounding: not-applicable: The status rests on evidence outside the model.
- Evaluator: exegra.evidence/0.1
- Scope: No model evaluation was performed. This rests on 2 evidence entities (ev-ms-trace-multiple, ev-ms-trace-self), of which 2 have a recorded execution result; the rest is author-entered.
- Explanation: Exegra holds a recorded result of executing ev-ms-trace-multiple and ev-ms-trace-self: pass. A claim is as strong as its weakest part, so it is not established. However, the assumption "extracted-facts-current" is recorded as refuted (the import scan found 14 file-system imports with no declared relationship: src/core/derivation-cache.js (derivation-cache) imports node:fs (filesystem); src/core/lean.js (lean-call-boundary) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/build-engine.js (rust-engine-oracle) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/check-spec.js (spec-checker) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/conformance.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/differential.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/gen-engine-inputs.js (formal-semantics) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/make-benchmark.js (benchmark-generator) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/prove-integrity.js (integrity-rules) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/prove.js (proof-export) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/record-goldens.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/rust-engine.js (rust-engine-oracle) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/spec-store.js (spec-store) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js (dev-server-plugin) imports node:fs (filesystem)), so the claim is undecided: it may still hold, but what it rests on does not.

**Holds only if**

- `static-extractor-sound`: The static extractor (tools/extract.js) reports exactly the static imports of the files it reads: every import, re-export and dynamic import of a JavaScript file, every import line of a Lean file, every mod and use of a Rust file, resolved to the file it names, and nothing else.
- `extracted-facts-current`: The fact file of extracted imports (specs/<project>/extracted/facts.json) describes the files on disk: the digest of every file of the implementation surface equals the digest recorded in it, by the extractor version that wrote it.

**Counterexample**

None.

## self-specification-split-by-area

Exegra's own specification is a manifest of part files grouped by area, none over 120 KB. Splitting it left the semantic hash and the document unchanged (entities of a section compared by id), a new component, relationship, claim, evidence record or behaviour that apply creates is written to the part the placement table computes from the entity (a component by its nearest named ancestor, a claim by the subsystem of its first subject, evidence where its claim is), one transaction's undo and redo restore every part byte for byte, the commands print the part file and line of a source location, and the specification guard refuses the part files and the directory that holds them.

## extraction-reconciles-with-declarations

Inside the extraction surface, static dependencies are derived from the facts extracted from the implementation and no declared static dependency stands beside them: no declared relationship of class static between components of the surface is redundant with an extracted fact, unwitnessed by one, reversed against one or of another kind than one, and every file of the extracted facts belongs to a module element. An extracted dependency with no declared counterpart is not a discrepancy, since it is the dependency. Runtime wiring stays declared and is not compared with the extraction.

- Kind: structural
- Method: extraction-reconciled
- **Status**: Inconclusive
- Check: structural: Decided by reconciling the declared relationships with the facts extracted from the implementation.
- Grounding: bound: Reads the declared relationships and the extracted facts (static imports only).
- Evaluator: exegra.structural/0.1
- Scope: Reconciled 591 extracted relationships between module elements (258 files read by exegra-static-extractor 1) with the 354 declared relationships at this model revision. Extracted relationships are static imports only: they say which files name which, never that the code runs that way. Runtime wiring (invokes, notifies, composes, ...) is declared and is not compared with it.
- Explanation: No declared static dependency inside the extraction surface is redundant, unwitnessed, reversed or of another kind than extraction finds, and every extracted file belongs to a module element (0 declared static dependencies inside the surface). However, the assumption "extracted-facts-current" is recorded as refuted (the import scan found 14 file-system imports with no declared relationship: src/core/derivation-cache.js (derivation-cache) imports node:fs (filesystem); src/core/lean.js (lean-call-boundary) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/build-engine.js (rust-engine-oracle) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/check-spec.js (spec-checker) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/conformance.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/differential.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/gen-engine-inputs.js (formal-semantics) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/make-benchmark.js (benchmark-generator) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/prove-integrity.js (integrity-rules) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/prove.js (proof-export) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/record-goldens.js (test-suites) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/rust-engine.js (rust-engine-oracle) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/spec-store.js (spec-store) imports node:fs (filesystem); tools/vite-plugin-exegra.js (dev-server-plugin) imports node:fs (filesystem)), so the claim is undecided: it may still hold, but what it rests on does not.

**Holds only if**

- `static-extractor-sound`: The static extractor (tools/extract.js) reports exactly the static imports of the files it reads: every import, re-export and dynamic import of a JavaScript file, every import line of a Lean file, every mod and use of a Rust file, resolved to the file it names, and nothing else.
- `extracted-facts-current`: The fact file of extracted imports (specs/<project>/extracted/facts.json) describes the files on disk: the digest of every file of the implementation surface equals the digest recorded in it, by the extractor version that wrote it.

**Counterexample**

None.

**Evidence**

None is attached.

## shared-base-single-commit

At most one of the two writes from the same initial revision commits.

- Kind: invariant
- Method: state-invariant
- **Status**: Verified within the stated model and assumptions
- Check: restated-scenario: `shared-base-write-flow.accepted <= 1` bounds the counter "shared-base-write-flow.accepted" by exactly what its one scenario "two-session-edit-race" produces (its largest value is 1): the bound restates the scenario rather than a rule that holds for other inputs.
- Grounding: self-contained: The behaviors the check reads ("shared-base-write-flow") refine nothing, are stored in no record, take no contract's events and read no anchored behavior; they decide on payload fields the scenarios supply (event "two-session-write" field "base", event "two-session-write" field "value").
- Evaluator: exegra.trace/0.1
- Scope: Under fault model Local workstation (5 failure modes; bounds: at most 1 failures at once). Checked every configuration of the traces of scenarios two-session-edit-race (2 steps). This is a check of these traces, not of all possible executions.
- Explanation: The expression held in the initial configuration and after every step of each of the 1 trace of "two-session-edit-race". Each of its 1 negative violated it: "two-session-edit-race" with stale-write-accepted at step 1. Other executions, faults or inputs were not examined. The result holds under fault model "local-workstation" only as far as the listed scenarios inject its failure modes: they inject 0 of its 5; the rest were not exercised.

**Holds only if**

- `machines-abstract-the-code`: The scenario state machines in this file faithfully abstract the code they describe: session.apply, Project.apply and its commit, the session's onCommit and flush, the renderer, inspector and source editor refresh and the workspace write.

## persistence-abstraction-refined

The save path's abstract behaviour workspace-flow is refined by its members, the persistence client and the write endpoint's abstraction: in the visual, source and rejected edits, running the members in its place, with the write-file hop hidden, gives the same boundary trace (events, payloads, times, outcome and the final state of every other machine).

- Kind: temporal
- Method: refines-composition
- **Status**: Verified within the stated model and assumptions
- Check: none: This build does not evaluate the method "refines-composition".
- Grounding: not-applicable: Nothing is evaluated.
- Evaluator: exegra.composition/0.1
- Scope: Ran visual-edit-flow (11 boundary steps, 2 hidden steps of the members), source-edit-flow (11 boundary steps, 2 hidden steps of the members), rejected-edit (6 boundary steps, 0 hidden steps of the members) with "workspace-flow" in place of its members and with its members, and compared the boundary traces in Lean (engine.refineBoundary): events, kinds, payloads, times under time scale 1, outcomes and the final configuration of every machine outside the region. A check of these runs, not of every interleaving.
- Explanation: Certificate: in 3 runs every boundary step of "workspace-flow" is realised by a step of its members (visual-edit-flow: 11 matched, 2 stutters; source-edit-flow: 11 matched, 2 stutters; rejected-edit: 6 matched, 0 stutters); strength conservative. 1 negative run breaks it.

**Holds only if**

- `machines-abstract-the-code`: The scenario state machines in this file faithfully abstract the code they describe: session.apply, Project.apply and its commit, the session's onCommit and flush, the renderer, inspector and source editor refresh and the workspace write.

- Scope: Ran write-with-stale-base-hash (8 boundary steps, 5 hidden steps of the members) with "write-endpoint-flow" in place of its members and with its members, and compared the boundary traces in Lean (engine.refineBoundary): events, kinds, payloads, times under time scale 1, outcomes and the final configuration of every machine outside the region. A check of these runs, not of every interleaving.
- Explanation: Certificate: in 1 run every boundary step of "write-endpoint-flow" is realised by a step of its members (write-with-stale-base-hash: 8 matched, 5 stutters); strength conservative. 1 negative run breaks it.

**Holds only if**

- `machines-abstract-the-code`: The scenario state machines in this file faithfully abstract the code they describe: session.apply, Project.apply and its commit, the session's onCommit and flush, the renderer, inspector and source editor refresh and the workspace write.

None.

## save-order-transfers-to-persistence-members

- Explanation: Transferred by safety-down-refinement: Safety over the boundary of "workspace-flow" transfers down a refinement: in visual-edit-flow, source-edit-flow the members' boundary traces agree with the abstraction's, so the claim holds there with persistence-client-flow, write-endpoint-flow in place of "workspace-flow". Not transferred for edit-with-disk-conflict, where the refinement was not certified (a fault acts at one level only, a member is opaque, or the scenario does not run "workspace-flow").
